ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u40	Prime Minister! Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] On behalf of the Government, Prime Minister Beata Szydło, the Government of Law and Justice, I would like to present to the High House a draft law on State aid to raise children - Family 500+. This project is a systemic, groundbreaking solution of family policy, is the implementation of our electoral programme, our electoral promises, our credibility, but above all a proof of solidarity with Polish families, a manifestation of concern for the welfare of Polish children and a desire to break the demographic crisis. High Izbo! A wise country supports a family. John Paul used to say that the family had a basic function. Man comes out of his family to realize his life's calling in his new family. The family is the basis for the duration and existence of the nation. The government's 500+ family programme is an expression of the concern of the Polish state for the fate of our community, its present and the future. High Izbo! It's a really important project. It's a project you've been waiting years for. [[Applause]] I am asking you to pay attention to this justification, really, of this absolutely historic project. [[Voices from the hall: Oh, dear...]] Yes, ladies and gentlemen, this is a historic project. No smiles of yours can change that. [[Applause]] By taking the helm of power, Law and Justice had to face a dramatic demographic situation. Poland has an intimidatingly low fertility rate. In this respect, we take 216th place in the world in 224 countries of the world. Since 1997, the fertility index has adopted values that characterize the phenomenon so-called. low fertility - less than 1.5. In 2014, it amounted to 1.29. This means that as a nation, we die. If we do nothing about it, it could be even worse than the CSO predicts, and it predicts that in 2050 there will be less than 34 million of us and, more importantly, we will be an ageing society. Great emigration, mainly young Poles, is also a drama. The data of the CSO are about 2.3 million people who live outside our country, and the latest data of the Institute of Statistics of the Catholic Church - up to 2,700 thousand. people like that. We can't afford that kind of loss. We don't want to, we can't, and we won't just stand by and watch it. Demographics can become one of the fundamental barriers to development. Poland, if it does not take care of the population, will not catch up with the countries of the European Union in terms of wealth. Fortunately, despite the dramatic demographic situation, we can break this deadlock. It is not that over the Vistula the decline of children is a natural process that occurs in richer societies, and nothing can be done about it. Luckily for us, the facts contradict that. First of all, in social research Poles declare that they want to have more children than they actually do. The gap between declarations and the actual number of children is one of the largest in Europe. Research also shows that material safety is strongly linked to the decision to have children. Similar conclusions can be found, for example. social diagnosis or experience of Poles, which in the UK or Ireland have twice as many children as on the Vistula River. High Izbo! These Polish children, kindergarteners sit calmly in the Seym and are absolutely listened to, absolutely. If I may ask the High House... [[Applause]] I'm so happy for you. [[Tricks like that are hey...]] Also in the last report of the Supreme Audit Chamber we find the results of research which confirm the theory that Poles mainly for economic reasons [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] They're afraid of growing a family. The first three indications on the expectations of the state in this area are: ensuring economic security - 41%, creating jobs for young people - 41%. , to help families in a difficult material situation - 32%. Other studies also produce similar results. Poles show in them that they are afraid to have offspring because of difficult material conditions - 74% - and for fear of deterioration - 61%. In Poland, therefore, it is the material barriers and lack of security that determine the low number of children born. Let us repeat: for Poles family and children are still one of the most important values. We believe that it is enough to identify and remove barriers that discourage Poles from expanding their family to emerge from the collapse in which we are located. The correlation between childhood and material situation and the external environment is common. In Poland it is particularly visible. The EU statistical office has examined the impact on children: GDP growth, consumption rate, unemployment rate of people of childbearing age, i.e. at the age of 15.49, and a sense of uncertainty in the economy. In the last three categories we occupy the first or one of the first places in Europe. For example, the level of correlation between unemployment and childhood for the European Union is -0,53 . In Poland it is the highest and is -0.89 . In other words, in our case, the deteriorating external situation has a particularly negative impact on the decision to enlarge the family. This can also be seen when comparing child rates and unemployment rates: the more unemployed, the less children. State policy has not been satisfactory so far. The trials were of an active nature. There was no coherent, well-thought-out strategy, as the Supreme Audit Chamber raised in its 2015 report. The assessment of the whole activity was devastating. The endless list of barriers discourages children, and the state has not done much to remove them. It did not do much to help young people - social policy turned its back on them. Suffice it to say that we are currently spending 2% on family policy GDP, and still in 2011 according to the Supreme Audit Chamber it was 1,3% GDP. This will change significantly after the introduction of the 500+ programme. Our spending on family policy is going to be around 3%. GDP, which will be similar to those countries that successfully pursue family policy. The experience of other countries shows that smart, long-term, strategic demographic policies are working. And we do not necessarily have to refer to the example of France, which is trying to lead such a policy. Among the countries that are so successful, there are also e.g. Ireland or the United Kingdom. You can also look even closer to us. The example of Hungary shows that courageous actions in the area of the population are effective. Following the introduction of the Family Protection Act in 2011, only 2/3 of Parliament's votes can be repealed - which provides for very important support for families, they show an increase in the fertility rate from 1.24 to 1.43. Estonia has similar experiences. In November 2001, the Parliament adopted a law on state family benefits extending the scope of family activities. Effect? This indicator jumped from 1.32 to 1.72 in 2010. Later, it decreased as a result of both the crisis and its withdrawal from the instruments of family policy. Someone will say that Germans spend a lot on family policy, and it doesn't work. It's true, except it's an exception to the rule. The Germans generally declare that they do not want to have more children than they do. It's completely different from the Poles. The world's and European experience shows that good family policies are effective. This is mentioned in numerous studies, e.g. OECD, European Parliament and Polish reports cross-cutting studies. They prove that the transfer to the family raises the fertility rate. The 500+ project is based on 5 pillars. The first is to say that people are the greatest value that you have. The strength, development, future and subjectivity of the state, as we have already mentioned, is determined by the number of citizens and their competences. The other breaks up with the belief that family policy is equivalent to social policy and that money spent on it is at a cost. Prime Minister Beata Szydło emphasizes that family policy is an investment. If we invest in people, we build a strong country. Poland faces great challenges. After 25 years of transformation, we realized, for example, that the model of our development, based on cheap work and foreign capital imports, can lead us to the role of screwdrivers operators, forklifts and suppliers of foreign companies' profits. This is often mentioned by Deputy Prime Minister Morawiecki. [[Applause]] The same challenge of the 500+ programme is to protect Poland from demographic disaster. The saddest reflection is the fact that children in Poland are most vulnerable to poverty, even poverty. The GUS data on this issue is appalling. Over 800,000. Polish children live below the minimum of existence, which means that their families cannot afford to meet basic biological needs, and more than 1 million are forced to benefit from social assistance. The most poverty affects the families of many children. Suffice it to say that in families with four or more children, the rate of poverty exceeds 25%. We read about the practical consequences of this situation in the publication of the CSO. The conditions of life of families in Poland. The data contained there is appalling. They show, for example, that more than 0.5 million children do not eat because their parents cannot afford to provide them with at least every other day... [[The amendments should have been supported.]] ...meat, poultry, fish or vegetarian equivalent. Nor can they afford to buy fresh vegetables or fruit at least several times a week. Definitely more families, bringing up 1.4 million children, admit that they need to give up additional activities such as tutoring, training for exams or sports, musical and plastic classes. There is also concern about health information. It turns out that because of the poverty of 530,000. children don't go to a specialist, and nearly 600,000. - to the dentist. The Central Statistical Office also notes that every third family has a problem with sending children once a year to a weekly holiday outside their residence, one in eleven cannot afford to organise a birthday party for children, one in eight - to go to a movie or theatre with children. Trust in Poles is another pillar of our program. By giving money to our countrymen for children, we believe that, as a rule, parents know best what to spend it on. It is not us, the officials, who should decide whether to spend this money on education or food. Of course, there is a safety valve in the program, which says that in extreme cases assistance can be provided in kind or in kind. This mechanism is needed, although it should be used exceptionally rarely. After reviewing the values behind the 500+ programme, let us look at its expected effects. He has three main, equivalent goals: increasing the fertility rate, investing in human capital, reducing poverty among the youngest Poles. According to our estimates based on the data of the European survey on income and living conditions, through the introduction of educational provision, poverty may fall by three percentage points and the risk of child poverty by 17 years of age decreases from 23.3% to 11%. This is an unheard of effect in Poland since the change in the system. None of the existing tools of family policy have been so effective in fighting poverty. [[Applause]] It should be noted that the forecasts show that the benefit of 500+ will mainly go to the first three income deciles, i.e. 30% the poorest families. It is in their case that the highest relative increase in disposable income is observed. Objective number two is based on the belief that family policy must be broken with the conviction that social policy is equivalent to social policy. Family policy is an investment. If we invest in people, we build a strong country. Already from the first research it appears that Poles will spend the money received in the program, investing in children. A better educated, nourished or treated young generation is a long-term benefit for us as a community. We reject the idea that Poles will waste this money without investing in children. We live in a country of normal, responsible people. [[Applause]] The programme, in our opinion, will also increase the fertility rate. Although it is extremely difficult to give specific numbers in such a situation - the decision to have children is made after an analysis of a huge number of variables - this gap between the expected number of children and the implementation of the economic barriers can be expected to succeed, given the Polish phenomenon I mentioned earlier. This is why the aid goes to the second child without income. It fits perfectly into what we are dealing with in Poland. We're becoming an only child country. 53% families in Poland live in 2+1 model. Most families have one child. On the other hand, we only have 4% of people who say they don't want kids. The Poles want them. Their first child should not be encouraged by economic incentives. The decision about the first child is not an economic decision, but rather a cultural decision, with which we have no problem. Poles want children, they want at least two of them, and they only have one. Our problem number one is not that Poles have one child because they have one child in principle. The Polish challenge is to change the 2+1 model to at least 2+2 model, although we dream of 2+3. [[Applause]] This is really crucial from the point of view of generation substitution, so that we do not die out as a nation. The educational provision will provide an additional 6,000. PLN per year for raising a child, which will allow to cover about 60% the cost of maintaining it. It's a large injection of cash that will reduce your fears... [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] ...for the economic future, and thus encouraged the expansion of the family. In addition to the three main objectives of the programme, there must also be at least three additional aspects. We compete with the countries of the European Union for people. That's where the high benefits are almost normal. For 32 countries of the European Union, the European Economic Area and Switzerland 21 pay universal benefits without family income surveys. All you have to do is marry Oder to receive nearly EUR 190 per child per month. In the UK or Ireland it is about 500 PLN. By introducing such a witness to us, we expect a smaller emigration of young people, and so we appreciate the young generation. [[How much? How much?]] So far, no government has said so clearly to the young after 1989: we care very much for you. [[Applause]] It is very important for us that you stay in the country on the Vistula River and build your future. We do not promise you abstract green islands, but we support what is most important to you: raising your children. [[Applause]] The economic impact of the programme is also important. Experts are talking about faster economic growth, e.g. PKO BP's analysis indicates that this year's growth will accelerate by 0.5 percentage points. We do not want to transfer money from one pocket to another, with administrative costs. The source of funding for the 500+ programme, on which Minister Paweł Szalamach was working, is to be fairer than before the distribution of tax burdens. This applies especially to large foreign corporations and financial institutions. We also want... [[The voice from the room: You did it differently.]] We also want the programme to be long-term. This is the only kind of family policy that works. [[Applause]] People need to believe that support is permanently included in the surrounding reality in order to possibly decide on children. [[Applause]] We want to pay every month 500 zloty net for all children from the second child and each subsequent up for 18 years of their lives. The first child is subject to the same amount of educational benefit of 500 zlotys, but after fulfilling the income criterion 800 zlotys net per person in the family... [[Gold! Gold!]] ... if a disabled child is raised in the family, the income criterion will be 1,200 zlotys net. [[Gold!]] There was never such a wide programme of support for Polish families after 1989. [[Applause]] It is estimated that the cost of raising one child is about 180 thousand. zł. From our 500+ program, parents will receive nearly 110 thousand for one child. zł. Of course, it is not our goal to provide parents with their educational work, but the help they receive must, in our opinion, be real, and it is really very high aid. The programme is to cost just over PLN 17 billion this year. There's money in the budget for that. Next year's cost... [[That's not true.]] Right. ...is nearly PLN 23 billion. This year, the municipalities that will be involved in implementation will receive a commission of 2% for its service, and next year and for each year - 1.5%. We do not want to commission municipalities without securing their funding. This security is good. The call for proposals for this programme will be launched on 1 April. Payments in the first period will be made no later than 3 months. The application submitted even in June will authorise the receipt of the compensation from April. We care about time, because many years born in the 1980s and early 1990s still come into adulthood. We have to do everything we can to convince them to have children. This is not the end of demographic action. The 500+ programme will not end the government's efforts for families. We want to build a broad horizontal demographic policy. We assume that first of all, it pays off as a community, second of all, we're not doomed to drift in this matter. You have to stop thinking there's nothing you can do. The experience of those countries that have succeeded in escaping demographic failure and our own teaching that, first, there must be no one miracle recipe to fix the situation, there must be a set of tools, and secondly, action in this area must be specific to the country concerned. What works on the Thames or the Seine doesn't necessarily work on the Vistula. The solutions introduced must be implemented consistently and in the long term and must not change every term. Actions for families, fourthly, cost money. You can't do family policy without money, but you have to remember that it's an investment, not a loss. That is why we intend to coordinate actions for families, they will be run by the resort that I am running and which, by no chance, has the name "family." In cooperation with the communities of practitioners, experts, local authorities and NGOs, we will review the instruments currently functioning in Poland in terms of their effectiveness. Then we want to put in place a strategic, holistic action plan to improve the fertility rate and to improve the fate of families who raise children. Demographic policy should cover all aspects of family life: from labour market, social security to social policy, social security, institutional care or housing. The Demographic Action Plan should become one of the main axes of action of state policies. I ask the High House, including the opposition, to have a good, substantive debate on the draft law on state aid in raising children, to discuss the arguments. I'm asking for your attention, not political fireworks. This project is the first attempt at such significant and long-term support for families, [[Applause]] It's a system solution. I ask you to work carefully on this project and support our bill. Thank you. [[Long-term applause]] [[Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice rise and clap]]

It will support financial independence and thus the subjectivity of Polish families. The bill is an investment in Polish families and thus in the future of a strong, independent, innovative Polish society. The programme also has an extremely important dimension in preventing poverty in families with children, and so far the most vulnerable poverty. Families in a more difficult material situation will also receive help for the first child in the family, even if it is the only child. It should be stressed that the new provision does not replace the existing arrangements for helping the poorest families, but complements them. It is therefore a real, significant change in the possibility of providing children with development conditions that do not lead them to vegetation in a closed circle of poverty. This effect also provides a higher income criterion than previously used for helping families in the Polish social security system. For the first time, we can also say that Polish solutions do not mean very low aid for Polish families compared to what we know in other rich countries of the European Union. As the Minister mentioned here, for example, in France it amounts to EUR 130 for the second child, in Ireland - EUR 270... [[And the first one?]] ...in Germany - 184 euro, in Sweden - 114 euro. I am giving this data to draw your attention to the fact that this type of benefits introduced in Poland are not an event. High Izbo! We must all be aware of one fact here - the family is the greatest good and is the foundation of society. The proposed bill is not something that should be treated as a complete novel. What is proposed will make Polish families simply afford to support their children without worrying every day about their future. This is our duty to Polish families, it should have been long ago, and it did not happen during the years of previous governments. Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! In accordance with the proposed draft law on parental benefits at the time of the adoption by the High House of this project entitled to this benefit in 2016, there will be more than 2,700 thousand. families. We want these families to be much more in a few years, because, as I said, family is the most important thing. I think that all of you who sit here do not deny the difficult demographic situation in Poland that we have been dealing with since 1989. Moreover, the number of people living in economic poverty is increasing with the ongoing demographic decline. It is precisely the solution to these two serious problems that we cannot and have no right to underestimate, that the Law on State aid to families raising children is aimed at. Ladies and gentlemen! The benefits under Law 500+ should not be treated as full coverage of the expenses associated with the maintenance of a family with children or as a social benefit, but merely as a partial contribution to the expenses associated with satisfying the needs of life and raising children. Everyone who raised children realizes that children are above all a powerful, largest investment in every family's life, but also a financial expense for families. Education, the preparation of children for life entails a huge financial burden for dependants, especially in multi-family families. The solution to the 500+ family programme is a necessary solution that will allow a significant reduction or even, in some cases, the elimination of this burden. This is a good change that will reduce the current economic constraints, especially among young people who want to choose to have children, but fear that they will simply not be able to provide them with the right conditions for life and personal development in the world. Ladies and gentlemen! In this Chamber, the argument was raised on several occasions concerning the heavy burden on the budget of such a provision. Let me remind you again what Prime Minister Beata Szydło said: A child in the family is an investment, not a cost. And in the situation in which we are in, as the High Chamber, we are responsible for creating the right conditions and introducing such a provision just so that in Poland young people want to invest - invest in the family, in their own children, in building strong Poland. We are aware that this project is only the beginning of a coherent and strategic family policy of law and justice. In view of all these aspects, the Law and Justice Club fully supports the proposed law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u91	Thank you very much. Please take the floor of Mr. Mark Michalak's Child Rights Officer. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u92	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen of the Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Ombudsman in relation to the government's draft law on State aid to raise children, contained in print No 216. The natural environment of the child's development is family. For this reason, it should be provided with special protection and care from the State, as referred to in Article. 18 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Also art. Article 71 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the Constitution indicates that the state takes the welfare of the family into account in its social and economic policy. It is similar in international documents, e.g. ratified by the Republic of Poland by the Convention on the Rights of the Child in Article. 18 ensures that the States Parties will provide adequate assistance to parents and legal guardians in carrying out their duties in relation to the raising of children. The additional role of the State is also indicated by Article. 27 The Convention on the Rights of the Child, which requires the parties, in accordance with national conditions and in accordance with their measures, to take appropriate steps to assist parents or other persons responsible for the child in the exercise of the right to decent social conditions and to provide material assistance, where necessary, to assist the family in carrying out its duties. Pro-family action by states should be a long-term investment in the future, building citizens' confidence in the sustainability and effectiveness of the solutions put in place. The Ombudsman welcomes the efforts of the legislator to support family-friendly policies by providing financial support to families in relation to the raising of children. The entry into force of the Government's draft law on State aid to raise children will realise both constitutional values and international obligations. The Polish state has developed many support instruments for families in recent years. Among other things, the new parental benefit granted on a similar basis to maternity and parental leave, the principle of gold for families exceeding the income threshold, the increase of maternity leave to 52 weeks, the amenities associated with paternity leave or the Large Family Card supporting multi-child families, as well as the kindergarten for gold or the free textbook - are only some. The educational provision referred to in the draft law on State aid for raising children is an important element of comprehensive and long-term support for Polish families, and is a significant complement to the functioning aid solutions. The Ombudsman also welcomes the proposed amendments to the Act of 9 June 2011 on support for the family and foster care system by granting a foster family and a family home for each child placed until the age of 18 years of the child's education allowance laid down in the State aid rules for the raising of children. The above arrangement, aimed at ensuring equal treatment of all legal guardians, meets the expectations of the beneficiaries of this allowance, rightly recognising that the child's stay in foster care should not deprive the right to an equivalent benefit. I also consider it beneficial to exclude income from the income catalogue for the acquisition of the right to social assistance, family benefits, maintenance fund benefits and material benefits for pupils. However, when assessing individual solutions, I signal the need to complete the project so that it fully implements the constitutional principle of social justice. In view of the need to protect the right of each child to decent social conditions, the Ombudsman sees a lack of objective justification for the different treatment of the first child in the family. The examination of the provisions of the bill indicates unequal treatment of families with one child, since in principle it excludes from the circle of persons entitled to receive the benefit of those parents, guardians of the facts or guardians of the child who exceed the income threshold laid down in the act. By assessing the right to provide education through the objective it is intended to serve, i.e. the part-covering of the expenditure relating to the upbringing of the child, including the care of the child and the satisfying of his or her needs in life, only the solution whereby the provision of education will also benefit the first child in the family, regardless of the income threshold determining the acquisition of the right to that benefit, will be to achieve the purpose of the law set out in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the project presented. High Izbo, there are no children worse and better. All children are equal and are entitled to the same state aid. [[Applause]] In pursuing the principle of equal treatment between children, the Ombudsman also points out that the consequence of the adoption referred to in the Article. 2 point 9 of the definition of a child with disabilities will be a situation in which a family raising a child aged 16 to 18 years of age with a slight degree of disability is entitled to a first child benefit if the income of the family per person does not exceed PLN 800. The solution adopted in the draft law leads to an unjustified distinction between families with light disabilities and families who raise children with moderate and significant disabilities. In the light of the above, if the High House does not accept the argument that it is necessary to support the upbringing of each child regardless of the criteria of income, it appears that the education benefit for a child who has a slight degree of disability should be granted in the case of the first child when the income of the family does not exceed PLN 1200. In addition, consideration should be given to taking into account the special position of single parents who are in material difficulties, whose income per family member slightly exceeds the income thresholds indicated by the project. I also leave the issue of adult learners to the attention of the High House, as in the opinion of the Ombudsman, legal solutions in this respect should be compatible with those adopted in the Law of 28 November 2003 on family benefits and in the Law of 7 September 2007 on the assistance of persons entitled to maintenance. [[What about vocational schools? What about the youth from the vocational schools? That's equality?]] At this point, it should be pointed out that, in a general speech of 23 November 2015, the Ombudsman called on the Minister for Family, Labour and Social Policy to raise the minimum income for those who apply for the maintenance fund. At the moment, a parent who raises a child who earns 1601 PLN net, is not entitled to this benefit. [[That's what the Child Rights Ombudsman says?]] Similarly, there will be no need for parenting when one child is raised in that family. Such a family will not receive any of the above benefits. [[No, I can't. Can you correct that?]] As a spokesperson for the rights of the child, I again signal the need to implement the aid offered by the State to single parents who raise one child by liquidation and, if this is not possible at present, to increase the income thresholds established by the Law of 7 September 2007 on the assistance of persons entitled to maintenance. The above should also be referred to the income thresholds for the acquisition of the entitlement to education. Financial support in the form of this benefit should not ignore those carers of the child who will not be able to comply with statutory requirements related to the income threshold, which will result in no assistance, even though the first child they raise requires equivalent expenses to younger siblings related to raising and satisfying his or her living needs. In conclusion, in the view of the Ombudsman, the introduction of another instrument to assist the family in raising children can bring tangible benefits. Providing children with decent, safe conditions for growth and sustainable development is a priority, and any form of support in this area is a step in the right direction. In the hands of Mr. Marshal, I passed on the detailed position of the Ombudsman of the rights of the child to the government's draft law on State aid in raising children contained in print no. 216 asking him to be included in the printing. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, I would ask you to consider the proposals and comments made in your position in the course of further legislative work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u475	Thank you very much, sir. Mr Marek Krzakła, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u476	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present my position on the parliamentary draft resolution on the immigration policy of the Polish state within the European Union. This draft resolution is bad, socially harmful and should be rejected, as the European Union's Committee on Affairs did at its meeting on 26 November. In addition, it concerns a relocation programme, which is not really being implemented by the Union today at all, and the Member of the explanatory memorandum has gone far beyond the scope of this resolution. Below in a few points I will present our justification. First of all, the resolution contains three false thesis on the alleged imposition by the European Commission of migrant quotas. First of all, no one was imposing anything on anyone. Poland agreed under certain conditions, which I will mention later, by qualified majority vote in the name of the security and solidarity that the applicants are calling for, but above all in the name of responsibility for the European Union for the adoption of a specific group of refugees, which was necessary for Greece and Italy to start fulfilling their obligations, namely to seal borders and register refugees. Secondly, the European Commission did not impose this, only the Member States agreed to help Greece and Italy sovereignly and accept refugees from them. And thirdly, the government has agreed to accept not migrants, but refugees who clearly require international protection. Applicants are mistakenly using the terms "refugees," "immigrants," and recently using the term "invaders." According to the Geneva Convention, a refugee is a person who has a legitimate fear of being persecuted because of his race, religion, nationality, belonging to a particular social group or political opinion, and is outside the territory of the country of which he is a citizen, and because of these fears he does not want or cannot return to that country. The only thing that deserves attention in this resolution is the call for a plan to help refugees and we can discuss this today. But the prerequisite for discussion is not to close our eyes to the problem of refugees and migrants, claiming that this is a German or European problem, because Poland has been a member of the European Union for 12 years, and today no country in Europe is able to solve this problem alone. [[Voice from the room: Refugees or migrants?]] The fact is that thousands of people are forced to leave their homes every day and seek refuge from death and violence. As a result of the war, over 260 thousand people died in Syria alone in five years. persons. We must be aware that almost 60 million people are currently displaced as a result of conflicts in the world. In Syria alone, more than 7.5 million citizens migrated inside the country and 4 million are looking for rescue outside the borders. The previous government, as a result of the negotiations, provided for the adoption of refugees with tough conditions that were included in Juncker's plan. Firstly, we have said all the time that the Union is unable to accept all and borders must be sealed. Secondly, we have called for a clear distinction between refugees and economic migrants. Thirdly, in order to ensure safety, we want to register everyone and establish their identity through efficient hotspots. Fourthly, we stress that it is important to fight more effectively the smuggling and trafficking of people, to increase humanitarian aid on the ground, to work more closely with countries considered safe, particularly Turkey, and, above all, to tackle the conflict in the Middle East. The Islamic State is a threat today, not a refugee. The lack of solidarity in Europe in seeking common solutions could result in the creation of the mini-Schengen zone. Minister Waszczykowski, who wants to fulfil the commitments of the previous government, knows that, of course, after meeting the conditions that we have presented. And at the end of the assembly. Europe is above all a community of values. Our task is to maintain Europe's unity, and the solution to the issue of refugees and migrants is certainly a test for Europe. And that's the problem we're gonna be dealing with for years. Therefore, do not threaten refugees, give up hate speech, and instead seek solutions together. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u490	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This year, on 28 April, the 80th anniversary of the decision taken by the Council of People's Commissioners to deport 70,000. Poles from then Soviet Ukraine to Kazakhstan. These are our compatriots who, after signing the Russian peace, stayed in the territory of the Soviet Union. There were over 1 million such people. Many of them lived in Soviet Ukraine and Soviet Belarus. The Soviet Union initially hoped that Poles would be able to Sovietize that it would be possible to create a Soviet man, hence various experiments undertaken, among others. the creation of a region, a tenant area and a carrot region, where our compatriots were to be Sovietized using the national language. When it turned out that it was impossible that Poles were still attached to their culture, religion, language, the Soviet authorities began the stage of repression. In the mid-thirties of the 1930s, the chase started, first media, then various kinds of weaker repressions, and in 1936 the decision was made to deport our compatriots to Kazakhstan. More than 70,000 were displaced. Poles from today's Soviet Ukraine, many people died both in Kazakhstan and on their way to Kazakhstan. Today, the inhabitants of Kazakhstan of the Polish nationality These are the 90 percent of the descendants of the people we are talking about in the High House. It should also be said that this was the beginning of repression, because a year later, in 1937 a Polish operation took place, which resulted in the shooting of a hundred and tens of thousands of Poles. It could be said, then, that paradoxically those who were transported to Kazakhstan were lucky, in quotes, because they were not murdered a year later. Anyway, they were very cruelly treated by fate, by the Soviet authorities, but later by fate and history. These people were not allowed to return to Poland after World War II and were not repatriated. Unfortunately, after 1989 it was also a group practically forgotten by Poland. Although different politicians, public people often had mouths full of great words, unfortunately in practice no effective help was given to our fellow countrymen in Kazakhstan when they came to Poland. I will remind you that in 2001 the act on repatriation came into force, but paradoxically this law instead of helping to return to Poland made it difficult to return. A year before this law entered into force, more people came to Poland as part of the repatriation than after this law started to work, i.e. instead of facilitating return, this law made it more difficult. And the case isn't difficult. The best example is the Federal Republic of Germany, where the Germans brought nearly 1 million of their compatriots, mainly from Kazakhstan and Siberia, to the Federal Republic of Germany in 1990 and 1995. We have not yet repaid this moral debt to our fellow countrymen. We haven't fulfilled that commitment so far. And I think that today, paying tribute to all those who were repressed, to those who were deported to Kazakhstan, to those who died there suffering because they are Poles, on this occasion we should also express the opinion of the Higher Chamber that, at this moment, one of the most important priorities is the repayment of this moral debt that we have towards our compatriots in Kazakhstan, against all the descendants of the oppressed. That is why the committee has undertaken to prepare this draft resolution and I am therefore asking the High House to adopt it positively and then to vote on it on behalf of the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u492	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present my position on the draft resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the 80th anniversary of the deportation of Poles to Kazakhstan. First of all, I would like to stress that it was very good that the Committee on Communication with Poles abroad prepared a draft of this resolution, because events such as those that took place 80 years ago in the Western Soviet Union must be remembered. And I'll tell you more, you have to commemorate such events. This is our national duty because, as Zbigniew Herbert said, a nation that loses its memory loses its conscience. The draft resolution presented to the High House reminds us of the fate of our compatriots who, under the terms of the Riga Peace Treaty, remained in the Soviet Union. Their number is estimated at around 1.2 million. Most of them inhabited compact areas in the area of Soviet Ukraine and Belarus. As Poles and as Catholics, they experienced numerous persecutions from the new Soviet power. The anti-Polish actions continued after the end of the Polish-Soviet war in principle constantly - only their intensity changed or the group against which they were conducted. Despite these repressions, Poles did not give up - they did not want to join collective farms, they showed attachment to Catholic faith, Polish language, Polish culture and tradition, they demanded unsuccessfully the right to return to Poland. All this made them one of the first to become enemies of the people and were affected by the repressions that preceded the very strong anti-Polish propaganda. It was particularly intense in January 1934 and lasted for many months. The anti-Polish campaign, which was then passed through the press, radio, theatre, film and even literature, can be described as hysterical. The anti-Polish action culminated in the adoption by the Council of People's Commissioners on 28 April 1936 of a deportation order to Kazakhstan of more than 70,000. Poles. The conditions under which the journey lasted for weeks were extremely difficult, but even more difficult was the new exile reality of the endless Kazakh steppes, meaning hunger, disease, and slave labor in harsh climate conditions. At the time, being a Pole meant an increased likelihood of death sentence, being sent to the camp, restricting access to schools and universities, free choice of profession, possibility of promotion at work. This condition lasted until 1956, and in many cases it continued until the dissolution of the Soviet Union. Here is another very important issue raised in the proposal submitted to the High House. There are still thousands of descendants in Kazakhstan who were deported there in the 1930s. They still feel Poles and many of them want to return to the homeland of their ancestors. Unfortunately, despite the attempts that have been made, they have not been successfully repatriated. This puts all the Polish governments under pressure after 1989, although the responsibility for this condition is, of course, differentiated. The Law on repatriation adopted in 2000 did not meet its expectations. In the period from 1 January 2001 to 31 December 2014, only a small part of our compatriots were able to return to Poland. It is probably a shame that more Poles left Kazakhstan for Russia, Germany and even Belarus than Poland at the time. It is therefore necessary for the Sejm to adopt a new law. It started several years ago, and it is worth noting here that it was a citizens' project, which was created on the initiative of the dead. Marshal Maciej Płażyński. Although this project received the support of all parliamentary groups at the time, the case did not move forward, and the PO-PSL government then decided to replace the act on repatriation with the act on the declaration of Polish citizenship. There's no time for these kinds of ideas today. Poles in Kazakhstan still live in difficult conditions - they experience poverty, lack of work, it is becoming increasingly difficult for them to keep Polishness. Therefore, the adoption by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland of a new repatriation law is a matter of primary importance as soon as possible. Without it, it will not be possible to bring our compatriots to Poland, but it is our moral duty to allow them to return to their homeland. Taking this into account, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice [[♪ The bell ♪]] to vote will be in favour of the adoption by the High Parliament of a resolution commemorating the 80th anniversary of the deportation of Poles to Kazakhstan. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u493	Thank you very much, sir. Mrs Bożena Kamińska, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, now has a voice.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u494	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Civic Platform on the committee's draft resolution on commemorating the 80th anniversary of the deportation of Poles to Kazakhstan. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Kazakhstan in the history of Poland and Poles is associated with the tragic Stalinist exports and the wandering life of our compatriots in the Asian steppes of the former Union of Socialist Soviet Republics. The Poles were the first national minority in the Soviet Union, which was subjected to group repression in the years 1936-1941 based on national membership rather than class membership. At the time, it was clear: Pole - the enemy of the nation. They were massively displaced from cities and villages from areas of Ukraine and Belarus to the Asian part of the Soviet Union, mainly to Kazakhstan. The basis of the deportation was the resolution of the Council of the People's Commissioners of the Soviet Union of 28 April 1936, which determined the number of Polish farms for export, estimated at about 45 thousand at the time. persons. Finally, the number of Polish deportees was determined for about 70,000. persons. The material and climatic conditions in which the Poles found themselves were extremely difficult. They were placed in a territory where there were no settlements before. Despite the lack of escape, settlers were subjected to a strict regime. Their status as specially displaced persons meant a restriction on personal freedom. Special regulations were in force to prohibit e.g. free movement. It was only Stalin's death and political change that gradually stopped the unfolding flywheel of the state machine. Although the Council of Ministers of the Socialist Union of the Soviet Republics adopted a resolution on 5 July 1956 to eliminate certain restrictions on the legal position of special displaced persons, special supervision of Poles was not closed until 1956, so after a long 20 years. To pay tribute to all deported, repressed, exiled and deceased on inhuman soil and supporting the program of repatriation of Poles from Kazakhstan, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, whose position I am presenting, will vote in favour of the draft resolution on commemorating the 80th anniversary of the deportation of Poles to Kazakhstan. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This process began six years ago. According to the assumptions adopted in 2009, all six-year-olds were to go to primary school as of 1 September 2012. This first proposal, which surprised parents very much, the community of directors and self-governments, met with very strong social resistance. Over the years, the government of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party has delayed the entry into force of this reform. It was moved from 1 September 2012 to 1 September 2014. Thus in the school year 2015/2016 100% children aged 6 years are pupils in primary education. However, since many parents were still unconvinced that schools were prepared to receive six-year-olds, and their children were sufficiently mature to study at school, the law provided for the possibility of deferring the child's obligation to study on the basis of a psychological and pedagogical opinion. Data on the number of pre-school education institutions - kindergartens and other forms of pre-school education - show that since 2013, the number of pre-school education institutions has decreased by about 570 institutions. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u550	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On 1 July, the revised provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure began to apply. The adversarial model has been introduced in place of the official model, a model that has been present in our justice system for decades. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u552	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I wanted to present the club's position regarding the revision of the Code of Criminal Procedure, as well as other related laws. The Minister presented the theoretical assumptions and pointed out the need to amend those provisions which have been in force since 1 July 2015. I would like to remind you that the purpose of this government submission is to move away from the principle of adversarial litigation towards the principle of inquisition clarification of the facts of the case in the course of the proceedings. Consequently, the burden of responsibility for the outcome of the process is misplaced. The principle of adversariality is one of the fundamental principles of the criminal process, but it should serve to build true findings in the process, not to create the possibility of weakening another fundamental principle, the principle of material truth. And this is done by marginalising the active role of the court, by the possibility of taking an evidence initiative only in exceptional cases, justified by specific circumstances, as is the current Article. 167 § 1 K.p.k. Therefore, it is necessary to restore the mixed model called the Inquisition-Contradicative Model of Criminal Procedure by 30 June 2015. And as the Minister said here, for decades such a model was in force. I would like to remind you that such a model was still in the 1928 Code of Criminal Procedure Act, which was in force until 1949, and as older lawyers remember internship and experience, the rules on criminal proceedings against minors were in force until 1982 until the entry into force of the Law on Criminal Procedure against minors. I think it is worth reminding that the author of this law of 1928 was Przemysl's lawyer Leon Peiper, an academic teacher at the Faculty of Law at the University of Jan Kazimierz in Lviv. The principle of objective truth was unchanged. She was still expressed in the article. 2 § 2 K.p.k., in its amendment of 27 September 2013 Only her normative environment changed and changed among others. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 5 § 2 K.p.k. there is no obligation for the court to seek to clarify all the relevant circumstances of the case and only if the existing doubts cannot be resolved in favour of the defendant. This is how the principle in dubio pro reo is defined. The evidence is always to be carried out by the court as a governing judicial authority in connection with the old Roman rule that the judge is the master of the trial, dominus litis. And regardless of the procedural activity of the parties to the proceedings, this is precisely the return to the fact that there is a formal review of the indictment, the content of the article. 337 K.p.k., return the case to the prosecutor to complete an investigation or investigation, Art. 345 K.p.k., commitment of the prosecutor to present evidence, Art. 397 K.p.k. The model of the preliminary investigation remains closely linked to the jurisdictional procedure. In the case of appeal proceedings, however, the appeal and reform concept of the Court of First Instance may rule and amend the judgment of the Court of First Instance. Any failure of the court of first instance may be rectified by a court of appeal which is fully entitled to carry out the evidence and shall rule reformatoryly. This is a good model and this model adopted by the Law of 27 September 2013 should be and is preserved. The amendments to the appeal proceedings shall be of a corrective, orderly nature. This novel introduces an extraordinary appeal in the form of an action against the judgment of the Court of Appeal. This complaint will be brought to the Supreme Court. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u556	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The bill presented is again being discussed about the model of criminal proceedings in the Polish judicial system. I regret to say that Law and Justice, following the principle: we are eliminating everything that has been done in the last 8 years, once again funding chaos, this time in the field of functioning of Polish courts. Chaos, the victims of which may unfortunately be citizens who defend themselves before these courts and come to their rights. Instead of improving or improving the solutions adopted not so long ago in the next area of the state's operation, unnecessary and fatal changes are planned. It is clear that errors or inaccuracies occur in such an extensive subject matter as criminal proceedings. However, it is not possible to agree with the argument made by the Law and Justice that these examples constitute a holistic picture. Moreover, the solutions proposed in the bill will not result in any revolution in detecting and punishing criminals. The main motive of the change is merely the restoration of old solutions, to which a large group of prosecutors is attached. In spring 2013, a draft of one of the largest reforms of the Code of Criminal Procedure was launched in the Sejm for 6 years. After 6 months of parliamentary work, including the operation of a special emergency committee, a model was developed not only to meet the current challenges of criminal proceedings, but also to eliminate many of the procedures that have been growing over the years to prevent a smooth investigation of the case. The fact is that this reform has been prepared in a rather hot atmosphere, but it is inevitable when such profound changes are made. However, the most important thing was that all parties to this discussion wanted to improve the procedures related to preparatory and judicial proceedings. A certain bone of discord remained until the end of the entry into force of the Act. However, I would like to remind you that it assumed a period of almost two years of vacathio legis, which is probably unimaginable for the ruling Law and Justice. However, a reliable analysis of the proposed draft law's solutions cannot be carried out without a brief summary of the new rules of criminal proceedings that have been in force for the last 7 months. One of the main elements of this major reform was the introduction of so strongly criticised by the authors of the so-called law. rules of adversariality. Its purpose was to change the procedural role of the court from the investigating body, which conducts the investigation, to the role of the arbitration panel, which settles between the parties at issue, which are the prosecutor and the accused in criminal proceedings. This amendment was a response to the shortcomings of the previous model of criminal proceedings, namely the duplication of investigations. On the one hand, the investigation, which was conducted by the prosecutor's office or the police, sought to clarify the circumstances of the case as comprehensively as possible and to safeguard the evidence for the court, and on the other hand, the entire investigation of evidence was repeated in the course of legal proceedings. This evidently resulted in an unnecessary extension of the entire procedure on several occasions. Let me just add that this solution was introduced in the 1969 Code of Criminal Procedure. The proposed model of adversarial proceedings was not an idea drawn from the hat. This formula is known not only in the U.S. or British system, but also in Polish civil proceedings. And none of those sitting in this room or listening to this debate will find that there is no truth in the British or American courts, and the trials are being carried out in an unreliable manner. Therefore, it cannot be agreed with the allegations made by the authors of the draft law that the introduction of the principle of adversariality constitutes an obstacle to the investigation of the truth in court. This is evident and unfortunately intentional abuse. The fundamental principle of material truth, or determination on the basis of actual facts, is found in the current Code of Criminal Procedure. It is therefore negative to assess the proposal to return to the so-called. Inquisition proceedings in which the court takes full responsibility for the outcome of the process and thus becomes an active participant in the proceedings. With this principle, it corresponds to the new wording of the article proposed in the draft law. 167 Code of Criminal Procedure, namely the introduction of an unlimited initiative of evidence of the court. It should therefore be recognised that the Law and Justice propose a return to a solution which could result in a breach of the principle of impartiality of the court. The proposal to delete art. from the Code of Criminal Procedure should be completely negative. 168a, which provides for an absolute prohibition on carrying out and using evidence obtained for the purpose of criminal proceedings by means of a criminal offence. It provides a certain assurance that no illegal set-up, no illegal wiretap or provocation can prove to anyone that they are being charged. The sad experiences in 2005 and 2007 show that such a provision is necessary. Unfortunately, the Law and Justice which entrusted the supervision of special services to Mariusz Kaminski, and the administration of the prosecutor's office to Minister Ziobra, considered that this fuse might be difficult for them to implement criminal policy, whatever the gentlemen referred to understand. Therefore, it is impossible not to recall the two recently adopted laws, which are extremely important from the point of view of this project. It is about the Police Act, i.e. the so-called. Surveillance bill, and D.A.'s law. I would like to remind you that the first one introduces broad powers in the field of Internet surveillance, while the second gives full power over the Polish prosecutor's office into the hands of politics, with the introduction of impunity in case of action in an undefined social interest. Thus, removing illegal evidence from the Code of Criminal Procedure creates a sad picture of a reality in which few people want to find themselves. It is also negative to assess the proposal for changes in the award of the defender ex officio as described in Article. 78 and 80a of the Code of Criminal Procedure. Well, the Law and Justice Reform provided the possibility of obtaining a free defender for a suspect, that is, already at the stage of police or prosecution proceedings. This is extremely important, because that's when the first hearings, the examination of evidence and the imposition of charges occur. According to the proposed bill, this entitlement is abolished. Thus, only the person who can afford to pay a professional lawyer will be able to count on the lawyer. Free assistance will only be available at the stage of legal proceedings, i.e. after the indictment has been prepared. It's a clear hit on the citizen's right to a fair defense. It is also critical to refer to the proposal to delete the article. 59a of the Penal Code, whose application is positively assessed. It gives the possibility to cancel the proceedings even before the start of the court case and, what is important, only at the request of the victim. This may be the case if the perpetrator of the act is reconciled with the victim, in particular as a result of mediation, and first of all he has made good the damage or compensation for the harm caused. Making the ideology that this provision serves the rich to buy their own innocence is, in principle, dishonest and puts this necessary and useful institution in a false light. It should also be noted that it is very often applicable to minor offences such as: fraud in online sales, where the most important thing for the victim is quick and effective repair of the damage. Ladies and gentlemen! The first months of the reform confirm that it is starting to pass the exam, as evidence of this is the reduction in the number of cases brought before the courts with an act of indictment, by increasing the possibility of voluntary punishment and at the same time not increasing the number of dismissals or acquittals, despite fewer indictments. Another thing is the satisfaction of prosecutors because they can handle their cases, because they know them best and know how they should be conducted in evidence. The next is a lower burden on prosecutors of the district attorneys conducting investigations, thus increasing the number of investigations directed to them. The judges estimate that there has been a reduction in the number of court cases needed to investigate the case and that a positive assessment of the participants in the process is obtained from taking evidence of secondary importance. Finally, I would like to emphasise that the withdrawal of the reform will be a step backwards. The judiciary and the justice system is not a playground where toys can be changed every now and then. This is a particular area of functioning of a state that clearly requires peace, reason and evolutionary change. Any other way will cause the most suffering to those whose courts are to defend and protect, namely Polish citizens. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Civic Platform Club therefore requests that the project be rejected at first reading. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u560	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Before assessing whether the proposed departure from the July 2015 amendment is justified or not, the two most important changes that the amendment has introduced should be recalled. Firstly, it was the introduction of the adversarial process in place of the previous inquisition. Secondly, what is extremely important, the principle of material truth in criminal proceedings has been practically abandoned. This meant that, from now on, it does not matter to the court whether the perpetrator actually committed the crime or not, but it does matter which of the parties to the trial will present better arguments incriminating or exonerating the accused. In conjunction with other amendments, the effects of such solutions could be disastrous. There have been many opinions that sooner or later this would lead to a situation where wealthy and influential people, who can afford better defenders, on the basis of opinions, expertise, would become unpunished, and poor people, without connection, would become helpless to the justice system. Over time, it turns out that there are more and more satisfied with the changes, both judges and policemen and prosecutors. Why is this happening? Well, ladies and gentlemen, thanks to the new regulations, police officers can get rid of a large number of cases by making them disappear at the preliminary stage of the procedure or refusing to introduce them, and no one will prevent them from doing so. The prosecutor shall no longer approve the termination of investigations in the in-rem phase and the refusal to initiate investigations, nor shall he order action at the initial stage of the proceedings. The prosecutors are running a lot less cases because of police bailouts that aren't responsible for them, and they don't have to approve them. Most of the activities that have so far had to be carried out in the preparatory proceedings may be assigned to the court by submitting a request for proof, opinions, expertise, hearings. The courts have much less cases, most of which include applications for punishment pursuant to the Article. 335 K.p.k. It can be accepted that the current rules make it necessary for the prosecutor to step up his activity in court and, in some cases, allow for faster termination of proceedings, but at the same time have many negative consequences, both for the victims and the accused. I'm just gonna talk about a few. The first such amendment concerns Article. 5 K.p.k. Earlier, before July 2015, this provision indicated that any insurmountable doubt would be decided in favour of the accused. This meant that if there was any doubt, it had to be clarified by carrying out all available evidence, hearing witnesses, expert expertise, and only when it did not succeed could it be decided in favour of the perpetrator. The current amendment deletes the non-removable phrase, which means that any doubt that has not been explained, even if it has been abandoned, is settled in favour of the perpetrator. This has very serious consequences. It is worth noting that, although this amendment has been introduced with a view to judicial proceedings, it also concerns preliminary proceedings. Back to court proceedings. One of the main objectives of the July amendment is the adversariality of the process of increasing the opportunities and evidence initiative of the parties. The judge becomes merely an arbiter to ensure the proper conduct of the hearing and not, as under the previous rules, to clarify the relevant circumstances of the case. Now it's the parties who are conducting the evidence. The defendant uses the help of a defense attorney, but he can also use the help of detectives to look for evidence, order an opinion, an expert opinion. It is clear that the accused with large financial resources is in a much better position than the average citizen, because in today's reality he is able to obtain the evidence he needs. If he can afford to bear the high cost of defense, he will receive it at the highest level. If she pays for her expertise, she'll get what she wants. The other defendants do not have the opportunity, and they cannot count here, as before, on the assistance of the court, which was obliged to explain all the circumstances of the case. A positive change was to introduce the possibility of amicable settlement between the perpetrator and the victim without court. However, it should be pointed out that the decision to remove this provision is one of the most appropriate intentions of the legislator. Art. 59a requires that proceedings be terminated at the request of the victim in the event that the perpetrator has repaired or compensated for the damage suffered. What consequences can this have? [[♪ The bell ♪]] many have already been discussed, and many have signalled that the new regulations prefer wealthy people. According to this provision, even the perpetrator who has already been punished may avoid criminal liability. There is an important point that cannot be overlooked here. No one checks whether the damage or compensation actually took place. Anyway, that's not gonna happen. I am on the clock, but I will say at the end that the amendments can really be blamed for a lot, because they have a negative impact, above all, on all citizens, both the victims and some of the defendants.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u568	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! I was honored to present the position of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club on the governmental draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws, print No 207. The authors of the project assumed that the adversity of court proceedings weakens the guarantee of detection in the process of truth, and even destroys the principle of material truth on which a modern criminal trial is based. Well, it's exactly the opposite. This is why the criminal process is supposed to be adversarial in order to strengthen these guarantees. After all, nothing is better for reaching the truth than a dispute between parties seeking to show their right. The projectors did not see that the numerous changes introduced by the 2013 and 2015 novels constituted a simple consequence of the withdrawal from the inquisition of the trial before the court. The selected corrections proposed cause an extremely dangerous lack of internal coherence. An example of this inconsistency is the appeal procedure, which is to remain after the planned amendment in a virtually unchanged form. However, its model was shaped on the assumption that the trial was adversarial. The nullification of this condition causes them to lose both the restriction of the rules of ne peius (prohibits of aggravating the situation of the accused) and the re-established boundaries of the court of appeal. It was not by chance that the September novel contained an extremely extensive and complicated intertemporal camera. This was due to the nature of the changes being made. The assumption that such a reform will be reversed without any intertemporal instrumentation is obvious naivety. It cannot escape the fact that the entry into force of the proposed changes in such a short period of time - only 2 months - will mean that, for a certain period of time, there will be three legal regimes in force in the course of the main hearing: the one in force before July 2015, the one introduced by that amendment and the one which will introduce the amendment in question. The project, which is important, raises a number of doubts about respect for human rights standards, in many respects reducing the level of protection in this area. There is particular doubt about the maintenance of the provision allowing temporary detention only on the grounds that a criminal offence is punishable by severe punishment. Such a provision raises constitutional doubts, and the project not only maintains this condition, but also extends it in the record that detention could be applied in the case of a conviction by the Court of First Instance for 3 years in prison, while it may now be so in the case of a sentence of more than 3 years. The Ombudsman draws attention to this. He is also critical of the proposal to abandon the need to invoke, using detention, the fear of obstructing the proper course of proceedings by the suspect or accused person. Another change that raises doubts is the repeal of the Article. 168a concerning the so-called the fruit of a poisoned tree, which is to prohibit the use in criminal proceedings of evidence obtained illegally. It is difficult to disagree with the Ombudsman's view that the application of this provision in criminal law is an additional disciplinary factor for the public services, which does not support the argumentation of material acquired in a way that fulfils the mark of a prohibited act, and that the retention under that prohibition does not pose a risk to the principle of material truth. It is difficult to talk about the good of justice when it is as if it is possible to obtain material for it in a way that violates the law. There is also concern about solutions to the reduction of the standards of the right of defence. Proposed departure from the Article. 80a the principle of establishing a defender ex officio only as a result of such a request, without taking into account the applicant's property and family situation, undoubtedly reduces the standard of constitutional rights of defence. In the view of the European Court of Human Rights too, the right of defence is a fundamental condition of the right to a fair trial, which is worth paying attention to. There are also doubts as to the lengthening of the time limits within which the authorities of the State may challenge the defendant's final settlements to the end of the proceedings and the limitation of the liability of the State Treasury for errors in the justice system. The project also assumes that the witness will no longer be able, like today, to give false testimony when he puts himself or his loved ones under criminal responsibility. The project also strengthens the criminal responsibility for a witness' testimony to the truth or for concealing the truth. According to the proposals contained in the project, up to 8 years in prison (today it is punishment up to 3 years). An expert valuer or translator who presents a false opinion or translation that is evidence could be punished up to 10 years in prison (today up to 3 years). The question arises as to whether this is not too severe a threat of punishment. Other proposed changes include: the restriction of consensus in cases of serious crimes which should be recognised at a public hearing and the elimination of the prosecutor's obligation to appear at a hearing in matters of minor importance, in view of the possibility for the courts to carry out evidence ex officio. A controversial institution of conciliation (restitution) is disappearing from the Penal Code, i.e. art. 59a. At the request of the victim, the prosecutor resigned if the perpetrator reconciled with the victim and repaired the damage or made amends. The benefit of restitution redemption could be enjoyed e.g. the perpetrator of the fight, the beating, the injury of the body, even the robbery or the extortion of the robber, if the act was less important. High Izbo! Legal Autorites are compatible - the provisions introduced by the two major amendments of 2013 and 2015 should have the possibility to embody in practice. This would allow for the diagnosis of real problems that arise from the practical functioning of new legal solutions. According to the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, it would be a far-reaching restraint in pushing for extensive changes of a systemic nature in the criminal law. We express our disapproval for the fundamental direction of the change in the model of the main hearing, which provides for a return to the inquisition model of the criminal trial at the trial, breaking the fundamental realities of the modern criminal process, the division of the procedural roles in the context of the legal dispute at the main hearing. Therefore, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, as it sounded in previous positions, is also in favour of rejecting the project at first reading. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u580	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Court fees in Poland are among the highest in Europe. In civil matters, when we want to fight for damages or unpaid invoices, we have to pay a fee, usually 5% the amount requested. The maximum fee is up to 100,000. zł. High judicial costs represent an important limitation on each of us's right to court. The system of judicial fees in civil matters is very diverse. These fees are not only fiscal but also non-fiscal. The level of fees per case category is a State policy measure on the impact on those who intend to initiate judicial proceedings. Low fees and statutory exemptions from charges in certain cases indicate preferential treatment of these cases, e.g. labour law or social security matters. This particular protection is deserved by consumer claims arising from banking activities. Against this challenge comes the Government's draft law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters, which was, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, addressed by the Marshal for first reading in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. Consumers to meet important life needs, e.g. the purchase of an apartment or a car, they must use the money offered by the banks, because often their current income does not allow them to make sufficient savings to meet these needs. The same should be said for producers of family farms who, in some situations, are forced to use the services of banks to ensure the continuity of the farm's activities. The amendment proposed in this draft law aims to increase the protection of consumers and producers of family farms arising from bank operations, such as: the granting of loans, the acceptance of cash contributions payable on demand and the keeping of accounts for such contributions or the keeping of other bank accounts. The catalogue of these activities is included in the Banking Law. There is a serious difference between the consumer and the bank as to the economic situation of the parties. The consumer is in a less convenient position. The need to pay a relatively high court fee and the uncertainty about obtaining and possible scope of exemption from that charge is a serious factor which may discourage these entities from pursuing their claims. Today, the Committee on Justice and Human Rights carried out its first reading and then examined the draft law. During the work in committee, one legislative amendment and editorial comments were tabled. Both the amendment and the comments were accepted by the committee. The amendment adopted aims to bring the concept of a natural person running a family holding in line with the provisions of other laws already in force in the legal system. The Commission requests the adoption by the High Sejm of a draft law. Thank you.
