ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u582	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice my position on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. We support the main aim of the project to increase the protection of consumers and producers of family farms who are facing claims arising from the banking activities referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2 of the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law. According to Art. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters shall be charged a comparative fee in civil matters. It is 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 100,000. Zloty, that's the rule. The Act of 25 September 2015 amending the Banking Law Act and certain other laws introduced specific provisions in that Law, according to which in cases for claims arising from banking activities the relative fee is different and equal to 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but there is also a difference, not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1 000. zł. And such regulation is justified with regard to consumers and agricultural producers operating family farms, because consumers are often forced to use the money offered by banks to meet important, often basic living needs, such as the purchase of a flat, a car. This also applies to family farmers who are only able to ensure the continuity of the farm's activities. The dispute between the consumer or the agricultural producer, on the one hand, and the bank, on the other hand, is usually a serious imbalance in the economic potential of the parties. Therefore, the position of the consumer and the agricultural producer is clearly weaker. The obligation to pay a relatively high court fee and uncertainty as to the outcome of the litigation is a circumstance which may discourage those entities from seeking their claims. Therefore, in our view, these entities should be given powers that facilitate the recovery of claims arising from banking activities. However, there is no reason for banks and other entities to benefit from specific regulation. The entry into force of the proposed amendment will result in court fees being charged at the general rates. For these reasons, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club through further legislative work on the bill.

Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a, concerning a comparative fee in proceedings for claims arising from bank operations, does not deserve support. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u588	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. At present, the provision of the article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a on judicial costs in civil matters shall read: In matters relating to claims arising from the banking activities referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2 of the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law, the relative fee is 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1000. It follows from the above that the relative charge in the proceedings arising from bank operations irrespective of the entity which has such a claim is currently 5% , but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1 000. zł. The proposed amendment aims to abolish the preference of banks. After the amendment, the preferential levy is intended to apply only to consumers and agricultural producers operating a family farm. Other entities, including banks, will pay a comparative fee on a general basis, i.e. 5% , but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 100,000. zł. This appears to be a fair solution because consumers and agricultural producers operating family farms are generally weaker players in the market compared to banks. However, the proposed amendment has a weak point. Namely: What if the action is brought by a non-privileged party, e.g. by a bank against a consumer or an agricultural producer, and this entity will win the case in court? The bank will have to pay the court fee on a general basis, so the maximum fee will be 100,000. zł. If the bank wins, the consumer will be obliged to reimburse the bank for the costs incurred from the amount of the court fee paid by the bank. When we put together the amount of the fee that is currently in force, with the amount that will apply after the amendment, the situation will be as follows. Currently, in proceedings for claims arising from bank operations for each entity, the court fee is 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1 000. zł. If the value of the subject matter of the dispute is 250 thousand. zł, this court fee will amount to 1 000. zł, this will be the maximum amount of the fee. After the amendment, the fee for consumers and agricultural producers will be 5% as above. the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 1 000. zł. So at the value of the subject matter of the dispute, 250,000. PLN 1 000 will be charged. zł., this will be the maximum amount of the fee. A fee for non-consumers and agricultural producers, i.e. for banks, it will be 5% the value of the subject-matter of the dispute or the subject-matter of the appeal, but not less than PLN 30 and not more than PLN 100,000. zł. If the value of the subject matter of the dispute is 250 thousand. zł, this court fee will then amount to 12.5 thousand. zł, which is 5%. The proposed amendment will therefore benefit the consumer or the agricultural producer only if the consumer or the agricultural producer brings the action. If the consumer or the agricultural producer is a party to the defendant's case and the process loses, he will have to bear the costs calculated on a general basis, without taking into account preferential rates. Ladies and gentlemen, it may be several hundred times more than it is today. As regards the revision of the Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a The Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters and on Possible Changes in Recovery of Process Costs for Banks is currently being reimbursed on the basis of Article. 98 K.p.c. Paragraph 1, which provides that the unsuccessful party is to reimburse the opponent at his request for the costs necessary for the purpose of the investigation of the rights and the purpose of the defence. This provision is of the utmost importance and there is no need to change it. The person who brings the action and wins the case shall be reimbursed, including the fee he has paid against the application. No distinction should be made in this respect, e.g. on the fact that the consumer is refunding the full fee on the application and only part of the fee to the legal person or, going further, to all entities except banks, we refund the full fee and only part of the fee to the banks. Such a change would violate the main procedural principle, namely the principle of equality of the parties in civil proceedings. On behalf of the Poselski Club I recommend to the High House that this proposal be sent back to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights in order to remedy it. Otherwise, the Modern will have to vote against the evil one. [[♪ The bell ♪]] And an unconstitutional bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u624	Statement on the 102nd anniversary of Tadeusz Sawicz's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! February 13th this year We will celebrate the 102nd anniversary of the birth of Tadeusz Sawicz, Polish brigade general, military pilot, participant of World War II, fighting among others. In the battle for England. Tadeusz Sawicz was born on 13 February 1914 in Warsaw. At the age of 19, he joined the Polish Army. A year later, he completed a unitary course at the Infantry Reserve School, after which he started his education at the Dęblin Aviation School. In the fall of 1936, he was promoted to lieutenant. Then he began the pilot's service in 111. Hunter Squadron. After the outbreak of World War II, Tadeusz Sawicz served as Deputy Commander 114. Hunter Squadron. During the September campaign, he was commissioned to deliver orders to General Juliusz Rómml to Warsaw. Then he received a response from the city headquarters and took the mission of sending correspondence to the headquarters of Marshal Edward Śmigły-Rydz. On September 17, Sawicz reached the border with Romania and handed over the documents. Shortly thereafter, Tadeusz Sawicz began training at the Aviation Training Center in France. It got through Romania, Yugoslavia and Italy. After the surrender of France, he fled on a Bloch MB.152 plane to Algeria. From there he made his way to Morocco and eventually went to Great Britain. He spent four months at a training center in Aston Down. Since the autumn of 1940, Tadeusz Sawicz served in Polish divisions in the UK - 303, 315 and 316. In the battle for England he fought in the Squadron 303. Tadeusz Kościuszko. In the following years he was in charge of the A 316 squadron. Warszawski's Hunting Division and 315. Hunting Division of Dęblin. In 1943, he became Deputy Commander 1. The Polish Hunting Wing, then he was a liaison officer in 12. The Hunting Group and the Air Force instructor at Rednal. In the spring of the following year, Sawicz began his service in American aviation, was assigned to an internship at Staff 9. The U.S.AF Air Force as liaison officer. Tadeusz Sawicz was in command of the 3rd Air Force at the end of the U.S. Air Force. The Polish Hunting Wing. Sawicz was demobilized in 1947 as a major. In the 1950s, he settled in Canada, where he worked in airline companies. He died on 19 October 2011 in Toronto. He was the last surviving Polish participant in the battle for England and the last surviving member of the Chasing Brigade defending the capital during the September campaign. Tadeusz Sawicz occupies 82nd place on Bajan's list - he was scored 3 shots and 3 and 1/2 aircraft damage. He was decorated, among others. The Order of Virtuti Militari, the Cross of Valor. On 3 May 2006, the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński promoted him to the rank of Brigadier General. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-09-sejm-11-1.u625	Ladies and gentlemen! The current situation of the Roma minority living in the Limanowa commune is extremely worrying, because at the moment the local authorities of Limanowa commune and the city of Czchów are in some impasse from which none of the parties can come out. The Małopolska Voivodeship Office in Cracow stated that it would not interfere with the move of the Roma to Czchow, because it does not find any basis for this, stressing that it is a more social than legal problem. The fact that a large part of the Roma minority is still living in very difficult conditions remains important. The condition of the buildings in which the Roma live leaves much to be desired. It should be borne in mind that the Roma family is often formed not only by small children but also by older people. On the basis of the results of the visits carried out in October 2013, which were included in the report entitled: The Ombudsman's activities in favour of the Roma minority in the Małopolska voivodship should be noted that the living conditions prevailing in most settlements visited by the RPO staff should be considered to be very bad. The condition of residential buildings is low. In most cases, residents are deprived of access to running water and sanitation, which is not only cumbersome for them, but also poses a health and epidemiology risk. According to the Ombudsman's speech to the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration of Mr Mariusz Błaszczak, signs coming from other localities, e.g. from the town of Limanowa, they also point to the local authorities' belief that the right solution to the housing problems of the Roma community is the buy-out, from the funds coming from the program, apartments or houses outside the housing estates, often in other municipalities. This initiative is of course beneficial for the Roma, if it leads to a real improvement in living conditions and is implemented in consultation with and with stakeholders. However, the problem arises when, for local authorities, this is the only acceptable solution, and in addition to the purchase of housing outside the housing estate, they do not consider any other investment to improve living conditions in the housing estates. The Supreme Audit Chamber published in November this year. Report on Public administration activities for the protection of the rights of the Roma minority in Poland (serial document.: LWR-4101-028-00/2014) also stated that the controlled municipalities did not sufficiently perceive and fully identify the problems of this minority as problems of the local government. For this reason, the initiatives taken by municipalities for Roma social inclusion were insufficient. I therefore agree with the proposal made by the Ombudsman to create a separate, comprehensive programme, independent of the Roma community integration programme currently being implemented in Poland for the period 2014-2020, which would only aim to plan and finance the process of improving housing and housing conditions in Roma settlements throughout Poland. In this independent programme, the issue of housing for the Roma minority in another municipality should be addressed directly.
